Which statement fulfills that needed?


SELECT state, city, SUM(salesamount) FROM sales GROUP BY state, city WHERE SUM(salesamount) >= 50000

SELECT state, city, SUM(salesamount) FROM sales GROUP BY state, city HAVING SUM(salesamount) >= 50000

You are trying to build a query that will return the last name, first name, and the number of classes to which each student has enrolled.

Only students that have enrolled in at least one class should show in the query results. The classID column needs show as "Classes."

Which queries will show the correct results? (Choose two)


SELECT lastname, firstname, classID Classes FROM students INNER JOIN classes on students.studentid = classes.studentID
SELECT lastname, firstname, classID NAME Classes FROM students INNER JOIN classes on students.studentid = classes.studentID
SELECT lastname, firstname, classID ALIAS Classes FROM students INNER JOIN classes on students.studentid = classes.studentID
SELECT lastname, firstname, classID as Classes FROM students INNER JOIN classes on students.studentid = classes.studentID

A report writer for a school wants to show students who appear in both a current student list

and an archived student list to identify potential duplicate records between lists.

Which query will return the needed records for the writer, including the duplicate records between the two tables?


SELECT lastname, firstname from studentlist INTERSECT ALL SELECT lastname, firstname from archivestudentlist
SELECT lastname, firstname from studentlist UNION SELECT lastname, firstname from archivestudentlist
SELECT lastname, firstname from studentlist UNION ALL SELECT lastname, firstname from archivestudentlist
SELECT lastname, firstname from studentlist INTERSECT SELECT lastname, firstname from archivestudentlist

Evaluate the sample of records from the following sales_summary table:



For a weekly sales report, a sales manager needs to see the top five salespersons by amount from the current day and the seven previous days.

Which SQL statement will fulfill these requirements?


SELECT TOP 5 Date, Salesperson, SUM (Amount) FROM sales_summary WHERE Date BETWEEN GETDATE() and GETDATE() -7 GROUP BY Date, Salesperson ORDER BY SUM (Amount) DESC
SELECT TOP 5 Date, Salesperson, SUM (Amount) FROM sales_summary WHERE Date BETWEEN GETDATE() and GETDATE() -7 ORDER BY SUM (Amount) DESC
SELECT TOP 5 Date, Salesperson, SUM (Amount) FROM sales_summary WHERE Date BETWEEN GETDATE() and GETDATE() -7 GROUP BY Date, Salesperson
SELECT TOP 5 Date, Salesperson, SUM (Amount) FROM sales_summary HAVING Date BETWEEN GETDATE() and GETDATE() -7 GROUP BY Date, Salesperson ORDER BY SUM (Amount) DESC

You have the following tables: student, enrollment, and course. You need to return the student name and each course they are taking. Students who are not enrolled should not be returned.



You execute the following query:



SELECT student.firstname, student.lastname, course.name

FROM student

OUTER JOIN course WHERE student.courseID = course.courseID;



The query returns an error.



How should you correct the query?


A. SELECT student.firstname, student.lastname, course.name FROM course INNER JOIN enrollment ON enrollment.courseID = course.courseID INNER JOIN student ON enrollment.studentID = student.studentID;
B. SELECT firstname, lastname, name FROM student INNER JOIN ON course, enrollment WHERE enrollment.courseID = course.courseID AND enrollment.studentID = student.studentID;
C. SELECT firstname, lastname, name FROM course INNER JOIN enrollment WHERE enrollment.courseID = course.courseID INNER JOIN student WHERE enrollment.studentID = student.studentID;
D. SELECT student.firstname, student.lastname, course.name FROM student INNER JOIN course, enrollment WHERE enrollment.courseID = course.courseID AND enrollment.studentID = student.studentID;

def calcTotal(taxable, amount, salesTax, shipping):
    if taxable == "Yes":
        subtotal = amount + (1 * salesTax) + shipping
    elif shipping == 0:
        pass
    else:

    new_score = score

    elif score > 2500 and rank > 2:

    else:

    return new_score

Share permissions apply only to local files, while NTFS applies only over the network.

No, because the Accounting group (Read) pulls him down according to the most restrictive rule.
Yes, because at the NTFS level the Allow rights accumulate (Read + Modify = Modify), and the Share gives him Full Control. The final intersection is Modify.
Yes, because Share always beats NTFS when Everyone is used.
No, because Full Control from Share generates an error when conflicting with Modify.
